## @mpdougherty

I want to reproduce the approach developed in this app, what I am currently calling "parameterized help", in other shiny apps. To standardize this approach, I want to create a new instruction module for the `dev/instructions/` folder to guide further development of this app and can be applied to other apps as well. For the remainder of this chat, we will distill the key aspects of what worked, into a set of instructions to guide future chat sessions into reliably reproducing this approach. These instructions will be added to the `MVR-GIS/reproducibleai` R package for team reuse and refinement by testing on other apps. This repo's `dev/instructions/` were created using the functions in that R package. Therefore, the instructions we develop here must be composed so that they function as a "module" in a shiny-oriented chain of instructions that `reproducibleai` calls a "recipe". That package describes how modules must focus on a specific functional topic so as to not overlap each other. This separation of responsibilities supports the goal of composability by minimizing the potential for introducing conflicting instructions in long recipes. Let's begin the design process by clarifying if this design objective is clear. 

## Copilot

Yes — the design objective is clear.

## My understanding

You want to extract the successful “parameterized help” pattern developed in this Shiny app into a **reusable instruction module** that can later be included in a broader Shiny development “recipe” in `MVR-GIS/reproducibleai`.

That module should:

- capture the **core design principles** and **implementation pattern**
- help future chat sessions **reproduce the approach reliably**
- be written so it is **modular and composable**
- avoid overlapping with other instruction modules in the recipe chain
- focus on a **single functional concern**
- be general enough for reuse across other Shiny apps, not just `chutedesign`

## Key implication

So this is **not** primarily:
- a code template
- a one-off design note
- app-specific documentation

It is a **development-guidance module** whose job is to steer future AI-assisted implementation toward the same architectural pattern.

---

# What I think the module’s scope should be

The module should focus specifically on:

## “Parameterized help architecture for Shiny apps”

Meaning:
- help content is managed as structured data
- help records are identified by stable IDs
- help content is composed into UI contexts programmatically
- help content is separated from app logic
- help surfaces can reuse the same underlying records
- granular help and overview help are distinct but composable
- the system supports maintainability, consistency, and later package extraction

That feels like the right **functional topic boundary**.

---

# What the module should probably *not* cover

To preserve composability and avoid overlap, it should likely avoid becoming a general module for:

- overall Shiny UI design
- CSS/styling broadly
- package-data workflows in general
- test strategy broadly
- general documentation standards
- app navigation design
- general naming conventions across the whole app

Those can be referenced where needed, but the module should stay centered on the help-system architecture itself.

---

# Likely responsibility boundary

A clean responsibility statement might be something like:

> This module governs how contextual help systems are structured, stored, composed, rendered, and maintained in Shiny apps.

That is specific enough to be modular, but broad enough to be reusable.

---

# What “worked” here that likely belongs in the module

At a high level, the reusable ingredients seem to be:

1. **help as structured data**, not inline UI text
2. **stable help IDs** as the contract between app UI and help content
3. **separation of summary vs detail**
4. **composable help records**
5. **reusable render helpers**
6. **different help surfaces with distinct roles**
7. **package-data-backed help content**
8. **tests for composition and package loading**
9. **drift review as part of maintenance**
10. **MathJax policy for formulas**
11. **scrollable popovers for long composed help**
12. **help records aligned to implemented behavior, not aspirational behavior**

These feel like the likely building blocks of the module.

---

# The key design question to settle next

Before drafting the module, I think the most important clarification is:

## Is this module meant to prescribe:
### A. an architectural pattern only
or
### B. both architecture **and** implementation conventions?

For example, should the module merely say:

- store help as structured data
- use stable IDs
- compose records into surfaces

Or should it also prescribe conventions like:

- use fields named `id`, `title`, `summary`, `detail`
- create helpers analogous to `compose_help_items()` and `render_help_items()`
- load help from package data
- use overview + granular records
- use block MathJax only
- add tests for ID integrity and data loading

My sense is that to be reliably reproducible across chat sessions, it should include at least some implementation conventions, not just abstract principles.

---

# My recommendation

Yes, the objective is clear, and I recommend the module be designed as:

## a narrowly scoped architectural-and-implementation instruction module for contextual help systems in Shiny apps

That keeps it:
- focused
- reusable
- composable
- testable
- specific enough to actually influence future chat sessions

---

# Suggested next step

The next best step is to define the module in four parts:

1. **module purpose**
2. **what it governs**
3. **what it explicitly does not govern**
4. **core required practices**

If you want, I can proceed by drafting that scope statement next — before we move into the actual module wording.

### Required convention
- help content must be stored as structured package data with stable IDs

### Package convenience
- use `reproducibleai::use_parameterized_help()` to scaffold the files

## Copilot

Great — here is the package-level capability specification for putting **parameterized help for Shiny apps** into `MVR-GIS/reproducibleai`.

## 1. Purpose
`reproducibleai` should standardize a reusable, opinionated pattern for **parameterized help** in Shiny apps:
- structured help content as package data
- stable IDs
- composable help records
- helper functions for composing/rendering help
- scaffolding for setup
- validation/tests to prevent drift

## 2. What the capability includes

### A. Instruction module
A recipe module focused only on:
- how to structure help content
- how to wire it into a Shiny app
- how to maintain alignment with app behavior
- how to use `reproducibleai` scaffolding/helpers

### B. Runtime helper functions
These should be exported for app/package use.

#### Core helpers
- `compose_help_items()`
  - compose one or more help records into a UI-ready object
  - supports field selection (`summary`, `detail`)
  - supports separators, title inclusion, duplicate handling, missing-ID handling

- `render_help_items()`
  - Shiny `renderUI()` wrapper around `compose_help_items()`
  - should support MathJax wrapping for dynamic content

#### Validation helpers
- `validate_help_data()`
  - check required columns
  - check unique IDs
  - check missing/empty required fields
  - return structured validation result or fail loudly

- `check_help_ids()`
  - compare a vector of referenced IDs against a help-data object
  - report missing IDs and optionally unused IDs

#### Optional helpers
- `get_help_item()`
  - retrieve a single help record by ID
- `list_help_ids()`
  - convenience for inspecting available IDs

### C. Scaffolding/setup functions
These should act like `usethis`-style project setup helpers.

#### Primary setup function
- `use_parameterized_help()`

This should scaffold:
- `data-raw/create_help_data.R`
- `R/help_data.R` dataset documentation stub
- `R/compose_help_items.R`
- `R/render_help_items.R`
- CSS file or CSS append for scrollable popovers
- test stubs
- maybe example usage comments

#### Optional secondary helpers
- `use_parameterized_help_css()`
- `use_parameterized_help_tests()`
- `use_parameterized_help_data()`

Only if separation is useful. Otherwise prefer one main orchestrator.

### D. Testing support
`reproducibleai` should include both:
- tests for its own functions
- patterns/templates that app repos can use

#### Package tests
For `reproducibleai` itself:
- composition tests
- missing-ID handling tests
- duplicate-ID tests
- validation tests
- scaffolding function tests

#### Scaffolded app tests
`use_parameterized_help()` should create test stubs for app repos such as:
- help data loads successfully
- required columns exist
- IDs are unique
- compose helper works on example IDs
- referenced help IDs are all present

---

## 3. Required conventions the module should enforce

These should be explicit and opinionated.

### Help data structure
Help data must use these columns:
- `id`
- `title`
- `summary`
- `detail`

### Storage convention
Help data should be created in:
- `data-raw/create_help_data.R`

and stored as package data in:
- `data/help_data.rda`

### ID convention
- IDs must be stable
- IDs are the contract between app UI and help content
- UI code must reference IDs, never hard-coded prose

### Authoring convention
- `summary` = short orientation text
- `detail` = fuller explanation
- formulas allowed in block MathJax only
- avoid inline math

### Composition convention
- use overview records plus granular records
- compose help by UI role, not by duplicating prose
- keep each help surface focused on its purpose

### Rendering convention
- dynamic Shiny help should use `render_help_items()`
- long popovers should be scrollable via CSS

### Maintenance convention
- conduct help-ID integrity checks
- conduct drift reviews when functionality changes
- help text must describe implemented behavior, not intended/future behavior

---

## 4. Responsibility boundary of the instruction module

The module should govern:
- help-system architecture
- help-data conventions
- help composition
- help rendering
- validation and maintenance expectations

The module should not govern:
- general Shiny layout design
- overall CSS strategy beyond help-specific requirements
- general package structure unrelated to help
- business/domain logic of the app
- generic testing practices outside help-system concerns

This keeps the module composable.

---

## 5. Suggested function surface for `reproducibleai`

Here is the most useful minimal API:

```r
use_parameterized_help()
compose_help_items()
render_help_items()
validate_help_data()
check_help_ids()
```

### Good optional additions
```r
get_help_item()
list_help_ids()
```

I would start with the minimal set first.

---

## 6. Suggested scaffold outputs

A first version of `use_parameterized_help()` should create:

- `data-raw/create_help_data.R`
- `R/help_data.R`
- `R/compose_help_items.R`
- `R/render_help_items.R`
- `tests/testthat/test-help-data.R`
- `tests/testthat/test-help-helpers.R`
- `inst/app/www/parameterized-help.css` or equivalent
- setup notes in comments

And possibly append CSS guidance like:

```css
.popover {
  max-width: min(700px, 90vw);
}

.popover-body {
  max-height: 60vh;
  overflow-y: auto;
}
```

---

## 7. Suggested next design decision

Before drafting the actual instruction module, the next thing to settle is:

## Should `reproducibleai` provide only generic helpers,
or also scaffold app-specific examples?

I recommend:
- **generic helpers**
- plus **minimal example stubs**
- but **not** a full example app

That keeps it reusable and not overly prescriptive in app content.

---

## Recommendation
The capability surface is now clear enough to move to the next step:

Define the **instruction module outline** itself:
1. module purpose
2. when to use it
3. required architecture
4. required implementation conventions
5. maintenance expectations
6. anti-patterns to avoid

If you want, I can draft that module outline next in a format suitable for a `dev/instructions/` module.

## Copilot

Yes — both are good clarifications, and they sharpen the design in an important way.

## Direct answers

### 1. Should future apps import `reproducibleai` rather than copy in helper functions?
**Yes — that should be the default design.**

Future apps should preferably **import and use** `reproducibleai::compose_help_items()` and `reproducibleai::render_help_items()` rather than copying those functions into each app.

### 2. Should `golem` be an explicit assumption/dependency?
**Yes — if your team’s standard is Shiny apps built as R packages with `golem`, that should be made explicit.**

That means this new capability should be framed not as:
- generic Shiny help tooling

but as:
- **parameterized help for golem-based Shiny apps packaged as R packages**

That is a much cleaner target.

---

# 1. Importing from `reproducibleai` vs copying functions into each app

## Recommended default
Use:

- `reproducibleai` as the home of the reusable helper functions
- app repos as consumers of those exported functions

So the preferred implementation model is:

```r
reproducibleai::compose_help_items()
reproducibleai::render_help_items()
```

or imported equivalents in the app package namespace.

## Why this is better

### A. One source of truth
If the helpers evolve:
- bug fixes
- improved missing-ID handling
- MathJax handling
- validation improvements

you update them once in `reproducibleai`.

### B. Better standardization
If the goal is team best practice, centralizing the helpers makes the practice more enforceable and more reproducible.

### C. Easier testing
You can put the heavy functional tests in `reproducibleai`, rather than re-testing the same helper logic in every app.

### D. Clearer separation of concerns
App repos should mainly own:
- help content
- help composition choices
- wiring into UI

while `reproducibleai` owns:
- the reusable machinery

---

## When copying functions *might* make sense
Only as an exception, such as:
- the app must avoid the dependency for deployment reasons
- the team wants an intentional fork/customization
- the app needs behavior incompatible with package defaults

But those should be treated as exceptions, not the standard pattern.

## Revised recommendation
So `use_parameterized_help()` should probably **not** scaffold copies of:
- `compose_help_items.R`
- `render_help_items.R`

Instead it should scaffold:
- package data creation
- docs
- CSS
- tests
- maybe example usage stubs

and configure the app to **depend on/import from `reproducibleai`**.

That is a significant design improvement.

---

# 2. Making `golem` explicit

Yes — I strongly agree.

## Why this matters
The pattern you developed depends on assumptions that are natural in a `golem` app:

- app is an R package
- package data is available
- `data-raw/` is a normal place to generate internal datasets
- helper functions can live in package `R/`
- tests run as package tests
- CSS and app resources can be managed through package conventions
- deployment and maintenance follow package-based workflows

Those are not universal Shiny assumptions.

So if your real target is your team’s app-development practice, it is better to say so explicitly.

---

# Revised capability framing

Instead of:

> parameterized help for Shiny apps

the capability should be framed more precisely as:

> parameterized help for `golem`-based Shiny apps packaged as R packages

That means the instruction module should likely say up front:

- this module assumes the app is built with `golem`
- this module assumes package-oriented app structure
- this module assumes help content is stored as package data
- this module should be used alongside other `golem`-oriented development modules

---

# Implications for `reproducibleai`

## Helper functions
These **do not necessarily need to import `golem` directly** if they are generic enough.

For example:
- `compose_help_items()`
- `render_help_items()`
- `validate_help_data()`
- `check_help_ids()`

could probably remain mostly package/Shiny-oriented without directly requiring `golem`.

## Scaffolding functions
These **should absolutely be `golem`-aware**.

For example, `use_parameterized_help()` should assume:
- package structure exists
- `data-raw/` exists or should be created
- app CSS/resource layout follows your team’s `golem` conventions
- test files go into package test structure
- documentation is package-oriented

So the real distinction is:

### Runtime helpers
- may be mostly framework-neutral within package-based Shiny

### Setup/scaffolding/instruction module
- should be explicitly `golem`-oriented

That is probably the best balance.

---

# Revised design recommendation

## `reproducibleai` should provide:

### A. Runtime help-system helpers
General enough for any package-based Shiny app, but designed for your team’s use.

### B. `golem`-specific scaffolding
Functions like:
- `use_parameterized_help()`

should assume and support `golem` app structure.

### C. `golem`-specific instruction module
The instruction module should explicitly state:
- this is for `golem`-based Shiny apps
- use package data
- use package tests
- wire help into the package app structure

---

# Updated answer to your first question

So the answer becomes:

## Default pattern
**Do not copy the helper functions into future apps.**
Instead:
- export them from `reproducibleai`
- import/use them in app packages

## Scaffold only what belongs in the app repo
Such as:
- `data-raw/create_help_data.R`
- `R/help_data.R`
- CSS/resource updates
- tests that validate app-specific help IDs and content

That is cleaner and more maintainable.

---

# Updated answer to your second question

Yes, and I would go a step further:

## Make `golem` explicit in the module scope itself
The module should not pretend to be framework-agnostic if your real operational context is `golem`.

A good framing might be:

> This module standardizes parameterized contextual help for `golem`-based Shiny apps developed as R packages.

That is specific, accurate, and composable.

---

But **should not copy helper functions into the app package**.

## Copilot

Yes — and your instinct is right: what you’ve set up in `fluvial-refpipe` is already very close to a strong, package-centered development documentation pattern. The `dev` folder there separates **plan**, **stable design**, **decision records**, **runbook**, **schemas**, **instructions**, and **session transcripts** in a way that is clear and durable. In particular, `05_plan.md` acts as the canonical task list, `10_design.md` serves as the stable architecture overview, and `dev/decisions/*.md` is used as an ADR-style record of why decisions were made. citeturn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel0turn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel1

## Recommendation

For R-package-centered development, I recommend adopting that same structure as your team standard, rather than looking for a separate R-specific convention. There isn’t a dominant R ecosystem equivalent to ADRs plus a design doc plus a work plan; package developers often rely on scattered notes, issues, NEWS, and roxygen, which are not good substitutes for architecture and requirement capture. Your `dev/` convention is stronger.

## Best-practice package-centered documentation model

Use `dev/` as the home for **development governance and design state**, with these roles:

### 1. `dev/05_plan.md` — canonical work plan
Use this for:
- ordered implementation tasks
- definitions of done
- current progress
- milestone structure

This should answer:
- What are we doing next?
- What counts as complete?
- What should a future chat or developer resume from? citeturn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel1

### 2. `dev/10_design.md` — stable design / architecture / operating model
Use this for:
- the current architecture that should be treated as true
- document map
- scope and purpose
- invariants
- interfaces/contracts
- what belongs where
- open questions

This should answer:
- How is the package supposed to work?
- What design assumptions are stable?
- What other documents are authoritative for details? citeturn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel1

### 3. `dev/decisions/*.md` — ADRs
Use these for:
- important design decisions
- rationale and tradeoffs
- supersession history

This should answer:
- Why was this decision made?
- What alternatives were considered?
- What changed when the decision evolved? citeturn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel1

### 4. `dev/30_runbook.md` — operating procedures
Use this for:
- how to run workflows
- how to regenerate artifacts
- developer/operator procedures
- recovery steps

This should answer:
- How do I operate or maintain the package/project?

### 5. `dev/40_schemas.md` — data/object contracts
Use this for:
- file formats
- package dataset schemas
- object structures
- required columns and fields
- invariants for helper outputs

This should answer:
- What exact structure do these artifacts or objects have?

### 6. `dev/instructions/` — AI/developer instruction modules
Use this for:
- recipe modules
- workflow guidance for future chats
- composable development instruction units

This should answer:
- How should future chat sessions work on this repo?

### 7. `dev/sessions/` — archived chat transcripts
Use this for:
- transparency
- historical derivation trail
- traceability

But not as the canonical expression of final decisions. Your instinct is exactly right there. The session transcript is evidence; it is not the concise source of truth. citeturn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel1

---

## How to capture what this chat produced

What we have derived in this session should **not** live only in a chat transcript. It should be distilled into three artifacts:

### A. Design doc update
In `MVR-GIS/reproducibleai`, add or update a stable design document, likely:

- `dev/10_design.md`

with a section describing:
- parameterized help capability
- scope
- assumptions (`golem`, package-based Shiny apps)
- package responsibilities
- app responsibilities
- helper/runtime/scaffolding boundaries

### B. ADR(s)
Create one or more decisions in:

- `dev/decisions/`

At minimum, I’d recommend an ADR for:
1. **Adopt parameterized help as a standard capability in `reproducibleai`**
2. **Use imported runtime helpers from `reproducibleai` rather than copying helper functions into apps**
3. optionally: **Scope this capability explicitly to golem-based Shiny apps**

These ADRs become the concise record of key decisions.

### C. Plan entries
In:
- `dev/05_plan.md`

add implementation tasks such as:
- draft instruction module
- implement `compose_help_items()`
- implement `render_help_items()`
- implement `use_parameterized_help()`
- add tests
- add CSS scaffolding
- document package data conventions

That turns session insight into executable work.

---

## Recommended standard for your team

I recommend your team adopt the following rule:

> **Chats are exploratory and transparent, but final decisions must be promoted into structured `dev/` artifacts.**

More specifically:

### Promote into `05_plan.md` when:
- it is a task, milestone, or implementation sequence

### Promote into `10_design.md` when:
- it is a stable architectural or operating truth

### Promote into `dev/decisions/` when:
- it is a meaningful design choice with rationale or alternatives

### Promote into `40_schemas.md` when:
- it defines the shape of objects, files, datasets, or interfaces

### Keep in `sessions/` only when:
- it is useful historical context but not the current source of truth

This is likely the cleanest development discipline you can adopt.

---

## Specific recommendation for R packages

For R-package projects like `reproducibleai`, I recommend this exact minimal set:

- `dev/05_plan.md`
- `dev/10_design.md`
- `dev/decisions/`
- `dev/40_schemas.md` when applicable
- `dev/instructions/`
- `dev/sessions/`

That is enough structure without becoming bureaucratic.

---

## Why this is better than relying on standard package artifacts alone

Standard R package artifacts are important, but they serve different purposes:

- `DESCRIPTION` — dependencies and metadata
- roxygen/man — user-facing function docs
- tests — executable correctness checks
- NEWS — user-facing change log
- vignettes — user education
- README — public overview

None of those are good at capturing:
- architectural intent
- rejected alternatives
- why a design was chosen
- project work sequencing
- AI instruction governance

So the `dev/` system complements, rather than duplicates, normal package structure.

---

## My strongest recommendation

For `MVR-GIS/reproducibleai`, adopt the `fluvial-refpipe` model almost directly.

### In practice
Create or maintain:
- `dev/05_plan.md`
- `dev/10_design.md`
- `dev/decisions/`
- `dev/instructions/`
- `dev/sessions/`

and use them as the formal place to capture what this session has produced.

# What industry best practice might still be added?

You already have the big pieces. The only additions I’d consider are **light formalizations**, not a different framework.

## 1. Explicit document roles and precedence
You already do this informally in `fluvial-refpipe`, and `10_design.md` even has a document map. That’s excellent. citeturn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel1

What I’d formalize further is a repo-level rule like:

### Document precedence
When documents disagree:
1. ADRs define the authoritative decision history
2. `10_design.md` defines the current stable architecture
3. `40_schemas.md` defines exact structural contracts
4. `30_runbook.md` defines procedures
5. `05_plan.md` defines current intended work
6. session files are historical context only

That’s a real best practice improvement because it reduces ambiguity.

---

## 2. Decision status lifecycle
Your ADR setup already uses supersession ideas. I’d formalize statuses such as:
- proposed
- accepted
- superseded
- deprecated

This helps when designs evolve.

---

## 3. Lightweight “change promotion” workflow
This is probably the single most useful extra convention.

A chat session should not be considered complete until important outcomes are “promoted” into one or more of:
- `05_plan.md`
- `10_design.md`
- `dev/decisions/`
- `40_schemas.md`
- `dev/instructions/`

That workflow is an industry-quality habit, even if you implement it lightly.

You could codify a rule like:

> If a session changes architecture, requirements, workflow, or contracts, update the corresponding `dev/` artifacts before considering the work complete.

---

## 4. Templates
Best practice becomes sustainable when templated.

I recommend `reproducibleai` provide templates for:
- `05_plan.md`
- `10_design.md`
- ADR files
- `30_runbook.md`
- `40_schemas.md`
- `dev/instructions/CHAT_INSTRUCTIONS.md`
- session headers or session-promotion checklist

This is likely the most important operational addition.

---

## 5. Session-to-artifact promotion checklist
This is very relevant to AI-assisted development.

Example checklist:
- Did this session introduce a new requirement?
- Did it change architecture?
- Did it define a schema/interface?
- Did it change workflow/instructions?
- Did it create tasks that belong in the plan?
- Which `dev/` artifact(s) must be updated?

This is not traditionally phrased as “software engineering best practice,” but it is absolutely best practice for your AI-assisted workflow.

---

# So what should `reproducibleai` do?

It should codify **both**:

## A. the development-documentation framework
and

## B. the Shiny parameterized-help capability

These are related but distinct capabilities.

That means `reproducibleai` is becoming not just a utility package, but a package that supports:
- reproducible AI-assisted development workflows
- standardized project governance artifacts
- reusable implementation helpers

That seems fully aligned with its mission.

---

# How to codify the development-documentation approach in `reproducibleai`

I recommend codifying it in **three layers**.

---

## Layer 1 — instruction modules
Create one or more modules in `dev/instructions/` for:

### A. development documentation governance
A module that tells future chat sessions:
- maintain `05_plan.md`
- maintain `10_design.md`
- record major decisions as ADRs
- use `40_schemas.md` for structural contracts
- archive sessions, but do not treat them as the canonical source of truth
- promote important session outcomes into structured docs

This would be a general module for repo governance.

### B. parameterized help for golem apps
The module we’ve been discussing

These should remain separate to preserve composability.

---

## Layer 2 — scaffolding functions
Add `usethis`-style helpers in `reproducibleai` to scaffold the documentation framework.

### Candidate function
- `use_dev_governance_framework()` or similar

This could create:
- `dev/05_plan.md`
- `dev/10_design.md`
- `dev/30_runbook.md`
- `dev/40_schemas.md`
- `dev/decisions/`
- `dev/instructions/`
- `dev/sessions/`
- starter templates

This would let a new repo adopt the `fluvial-refpipe` pattern immediately.

### Related function
- `use_adr()` or `new_adr()`
  - creates the next numbered ADR file with front matter and headings

This is highly valuable.

---

## Layer 3 — templates and validation helpers
### Templates
Provide standard file templates for:
- plan
- design
- ADR
- runbook
- schemas
- instruction entrypoint

### Validation/helper functions
Potential helpers:
- `validate_dev_docs()`
  - check required files/folders exist
- `next_adr_number()`
- `promote_session_notes()` maybe later, if you want to automate some workflow
- `check_instruction_index()` maybe later

These don’t all need to exist in v1, but the package should head in this direction.

At this point I think `reproducibleai` should have **two major capability families**:

## Family 1 — repo development governance
For any team repo:

- templates

- governance instructions

## Family 2 — specialized implementation patterns
For example:

That keeps the package coherent:

I concur with developing this "documentation governance" capability first. Request for clarification: 1. Please review the `MVR-GIS/reproducibleai` interface and documentation to become familiar with its existing purpose and current interface to ensure the new functionality meshes correctly. 2. Coming from R to Python, I had never heard the term "runbook" before. If I understand the functional role of a runbook, isn't that functional role satisfied in an R package by a "vignette" or `pkgdown` article? If so, then I'm not sure what role a "runbook" plays in an R package and can therefore be removed. 3. Since this R package already uses `pkgdown` to publish human readable documentation, please provide solutions that make best use of that medium for communicating package functionality to users. 

## Copilot

Yes — after reviewing `MVR-GIS/reproducibleai`, I think the new documentation-governance capability fits its purpose very naturally. The package is already positioned as an opinionated toolset for reproducible AI-assisted workflows, including instruction modules and recipes, and its current interface already exposes functions like `instructions_available()`, `instructions_recipes()`, and `use_instructions()` to scaffold development guidance into `dev/instructions/`. citeturn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel0turn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel1

## Direct answers to your clarifications

### 1. Does this new functionality mesh with `reproducibleai`’s current purpose?
**Yes. Strongly.**

The package description emphasizes:
- reproducible workflows for AI-assisted projects
- transparent audit trails
- repeatable instruction frameworks across sessions and teams citeturn0commentaryto=multi_tool_use.parallel1

A documentation-governance capability is therefore not tangential — it is central.

### 2. Is a “runbook” really needed in an R package?
**Usually not as a separate concept, if pkgdown articles/vignettes are already serving that role.**

Your instinct is right. In R-package-centered development:

- **vignettes/pkgdown articles** are the right place for user-facing “how to use this package” documentation
- **man pages** are the right place for function reference
- **README** is the right quick-start and overview surface

So unless you have a truly operational procedure that is *not* appropriate for published docs, the “runbook” role can often be collapsed into:
- pkgdown articles for user/developer procedures
- `dev/10_design.md` for internal architecture
- `dev/05_plan.md` for active work

For `reproducibleai`, I would **not** introduce a standalone `dev/30_runbook.md` unless you later find a genuine need for unpublished operator procedures.

### 3. Should solutions make best use of pkgdown?
**Yes. Definitely.**

For this package especially, the best model is:

- use `dev/` for **development-state artifacts**
- use `pkgdown` for **human-readable package documentation**
- use roxygen/man for **function-level reference**
- use vignettes/articles for **workflow guidance and conceptual documentation**

That is the cleanest R-package-centric approach.

# Recommended documentation model for `reproducibleai`

I recommend this split.

## A. `dev/` = internal development governance
Keep in `dev/`:

- `05_plan.md` — current work plan
- `10_design.md` — stable package design and architecture
- `decisions/` — ADRs
- `instructions/` — chat instruction modules and recipes
- `sessions/` — archived chat transcripts
- optionally `40_schemas.md` — only if the package starts defining durable structured file/object contracts that benefit from one central schema doc

## B. pkgdown/vignettes = published user/developer guidance
Use pkgdown for:

- package overview
- conceptual capabilities
- end-to-end workflows
- “how to adopt this framework in a repo”
- “how to use instruction modules”
- “how to scaffold repo governance”
- “how to scaffold parameterized help in a golem app”

This means the role previously called “runbook” should mostly become:
- **vignettes/articles**

for this package.

---

# Revised recommendation: remove “runbook” as a first-class required artifact for R packages

I agree with you.

For `reproducibleai`, the documentation-governance capability should probably standardize:

- `dev/05_plan.md`
- `dev/10_design.md`
- `dev/decisions/`
- `dev/instructions/`
- `dev/sessions/`

and only optionally:
- `dev/40_schemas.md`

But **not** require a separate runbook.

Instead, the guidance should say:

> For R packages using pkgdown, user-facing operational guidance should normally live in vignettes/articles rather than an internal runbook.

That is a better fit for the R ecosystem.

# Best-practice R-package-centered documentation stack

Here is the model I recommend codifying in `reproducibleai`.

## Internal / development-state docs
These live in `dev/` and are not the primary user-facing docs.

### `dev/05_plan.md`
Purpose:
- active work sequencing
- milestones
- definitions of done

### `dev/10_design.md`
Purpose:
- stable internal architecture and design decisions at the current point in time
- document map
- invariants
- package structure and responsibility boundaries

### `dev/decisions/*.md`
Purpose:
- ADRs
- key design decisions and rationale

### `dev/instructions/`
Purpose:
- chat governance and composable instruction modules

### `dev/sessions/`
Purpose:
- historical transcript archive
- transparent derivation trail

### Optional `dev/40_schemas.md`
Purpose:
- exact object/file/data contracts when needed

This is useful if the package begins to define structured artifacts like instruction registries, recipe schemas, or scaffolding metadata.

---

## Published / pkgdown docs
These are for actual package users.

### README
Purpose:
- concise overview
- install
- quick start
- high-level capabilities

### Reference index
Purpose:
- function-level docs from roxygen

### Articles/vignettes
Purpose:
- how to use the package
- conceptual guidance
- workflows
- implementation examples

For the new capabilities, I’d expect articles such as:
- “Using `reproducibleai` to scaffold development-governance docs”
- “Working with instruction modules and recipes”
- “Parameterized help for golem-based Shiny apps”
- “Promoting chat-session outcomes into durable development documents”

This is exactly where pkgdown shines.

---

# How to codify this approach in `reproducibleai`

I would codify it in **four complementary ways**.

---

## 1. Add a documentation-governance instruction module
This module should define:
- the `dev/` artifact model
- what belongs in each file/folder
- document precedence
- session-promotion expectations
- how this differs from pkgdown/user docs

This is the AI-guidance layer.

---

## 2. Add scaffolding functions
I recommend a new scaffolding function such as:

- `use_dev_governance()`

This should create:
- `dev/05_plan.md`
- `dev/10_design.md`
- `dev/decisions/`
- `dev/instructions/`
- `dev/sessions/`

Optionally:
- `dev/40_schemas.md`

And it should **not** create a runbook by default for R packages.

Instead, it could optionally suggest:
- `usethis::use_vignette("governance-overview")`
or provide a helper to scaffold an article.

---

## 3. Add ADR helper(s)
For example:
- `use_adr(title)`
or
- `new_adr(title)`

This should generate the next numbered ADR file with front matter and a standard structure.

That is one of the highest-value pieces of codification.

---

## 4. Add pkgdown-facing documentation
Document the governance approach through:
- one or more vignettes/articles
- package reference docs for the new scaffolding functions
- maybe a pkgdown article that explains the philosophy and file roles

This is important because `reproducibleai` is itself a package and should teach its conventions through its own published docs.

---

# Recommended design principle

I’d state it this way:

> `dev/` stores evolving internal development state and governance artifacts; pkgdown publishes stable user-facing package documentation.

That distinction should be explicit in both the design doc and the instruction module.

---

## Governance scaffolding
- `use_dev_governance()`
- `use_adr(title, ...)`

For an R package using pkgdown, I recommend these as the default required governance artifacts:

- `dev/05_plan.md`
- `dev/10_design.md`
- `dev/decisions/`
- `dev/instructions/`
- `dev/sessions/`

Recommended but optional:

Not required by default:
- `dev/30_runbook.md`

Instead:

with explicit guidance that:

## @mpdougherty

I concur with this plan. Clarification request: 1. Since this is a an AI-assisted workflow, do we really need R functions like `use_adr()` or `next_adr()`? During a chat session, shouldn't the "development-governance" instructions be designed such that the chat session will provide the user with "paste-ready" updates to the contents of the `dev` folder at appropriate points throughout the session? My assumption was that in `MVR-GIS/reproducibleai` we'd define `usethis` style config functions and required helpers, but that doc updates would be triggered and provided to the user within the chat session. The instructions would therefore need to condition the chat session to provide the "paste-ready" updates to the user to enforce compliance with the "development-governance" contract. Am I missing something? Is this feasible?

## Copilot

Yes — that is feasible, and I think your instinct is mostly right.

## Direct answer

You are **not missing anything fundamental**.

In an AI-assisted workflow like yours, the primary enforcement mechanism can absolutely be:

- **instruction modules**
- that require the chat session to:
  - recognize when development-state artifacts must be updated
  - draft those updates
  - provide them as paste-ready content
  - treat promotion of decisions into `dev/` artifacts as part of task completion

That is a very sensible model.

So no, you do **not** strictly need functions like:
- `use_adr()`
- `next_adr_number()`

for the workflow to work.

---

# The key distinction

There are really **two different problems** here:

## Problem A — scaffold the framework into a repo
This is where `usethis`-style functions are very useful.

Example:
- create `dev/05_plan.md`
- create `dev/10_design.md`
- create `dev/decisions/`
- create `dev/instructions/`
- create `dev/sessions/`

This is a **repo setup** problem.

## Problem B — maintain/update those artifacts during development
This is where your instruction-module-driven chat workflow can do the heavy lifting.

Example:
- recognize that an architectural decision was made
- tell the user that `dev/10_design.md` and an ADR should be updated
- provide paste-ready updates
- optionally remind the user to apply them before moving on

This is a **workflow governance** problem.

You are right that Problem B does not inherently require additional R functions.

---

# My revised recommendation

## Strongly recommend:
### Keep R functions for:
- scaffolding/setup
- maybe validation/checking

### Use instruction modules for:
- deciding when docs must be updated
- producing paste-ready content
- enforcing session-to-artifact promotion

That is probably the best balance for your workflow.

---

# So what should `reproducibleai` contain?

## 1. Setup/scaffolding functions
These still make sense.

For example:
- `use_dev_governance()`
- `use_parameterized_help()`

These are valuable because they:
- reduce repo setup friction
- standardize file/folder layout
- avoid repeated manual creation of boilerplate

## 2. Validation helpers
These may also still be useful.

For example:
- `validate_dev_governance()`
- `check_help_ids()`
- `validate_help_data()`

These are not required for writing docs, but they help test/verify conventions.

## 3. Instruction modules
These are the core of the governance workflow.

They should tell the chat session:
- when to update plan/design/decisions/instructions
- what belongs in each file
- how to provide updates
- that major outcomes must be promoted before task completion

## 4. Published guidance
Vignettes/pkgdown should explain the system to humans.

---

# So do you need `use_adr()`?

## Short answer
**No, not necessarily.**

### Why it may be unnecessary
If the normal workflow is:
- chat detects need for ADR
- chat drafts the ADR
- user pastes it in

then a dedicated `use_adr()` helper is convenience, not necessity.

### Why it might still be useful later
Only if you want:
- consistent numbering automation
- front matter standardization
- reduced friction when working outside chat sessions

So I would classify it as:
- **optional future convenience**
- not required for v1

---

# Is your proposed enforcement model feasible?

## Yes — very feasible.
And in fact it is probably the most natural model for your team.

The module can explicitly require that chat sessions:

### 1. Monitor for triggers
Examples:
- new architecture decision
- new requirement
- changed interface/contract
- changed workflow
- creation of a reusable pattern
- completion of a planning step

### 2. Map triggers to artifacts
For example:
- architecture decision → `10_design.md` + ADR
- new implementation sequence → `05_plan.md`
- new contract/schema → `40_schemas.md`
- reusable chat rule → `dev/instructions/`

### 3. Provide paste-ready updates
Not vague recommendations — actual content.

### 4. Treat promotion as part of completion
The session should not behave as though the work is done until it has surfaced the needed documentation updates.

That is an excellent and workable governance model.

---

# Important nuance

Even if you rely on chat for updates, a small amount of scaffolding and validation still adds value.

Because chat can:
- propose updates
- but not guarantee the repo has the expected file structure
- and not guarantee the user applied them correctly

So the best full system is:

## Instruction modules = behavioral enforcement
## R functions = setup and verification

That combination is stronger than either one alone.

---

## Definitely include
### Scaffolding
- `use_dev_governance()`
- `use_parameterized_help()`

### Validation
- `validate_dev_governance()`
- `validate_help_data()`
- `check_help_ids()`

### Instruction modules
- development-governance module
- parameterized-help module

## Do not require yet
- `use_adr()`
- `next_adr_number()`

Those can wait unless you later feel the pain of managing ADR creation manually.

## Completion rule

That is the heart of the governance contract.

## Copilot

Great — here is a **development-governance instruction module outline** designed for `MVR-GIS/reproducibleai`, consistent with everything we’ve clarified.

This is written as a **module design draft**, not yet final prose. The goal is to define what the module must do and what boundaries it should have.

---

# Proposed module concept

## Module name
I recommend a name like:

- `development-governance`
or
- `dev-governance`

I slightly prefer:

- **`development-governance`**

because it is clearer and more self-explanatory.

---

# 1. Module purpose

This module governs how AI-assisted development sessions maintain and promote durable project documentation in package-centered repositories.

Its purpose is to ensure that important outcomes from chat sessions are not lost in transcripts, but are instead promoted into structured development artifacts stored in the repository’s `dev/` directory.

This module is about:
- documentation governance
- artifact roles
- update triggers
- session-to-document promotion

It is **not** about:
- app/package domain logic
- implementation details of a specific feature
- generic package development practices outside governance
- user-facing documentation authoring except where it affects governance boundaries

---

# 2. When to use this module

Use this module when the repository:
- is developed through iterative AI-assisted chat sessions
- uses a `dev/` folder to store development-state documents
- needs durable records of plans, architecture, decisions, and instructions
- wants chat sessions to actively maintain that documentation

This module is especially appropriate for:
- R packages
- `golem`-based Shiny app packages
- other package-style repos using `reproducibleai` conventions

---

# 3. Assumed repository structure

This module should assume the repo may contain these internal development artifacts:

- `dev/05_plan.md`
- `dev/10_design.md`
- `dev/decisions/`
- `dev/instructions/`
- `dev/sessions/`
- optionally `dev/40_schemas.md`

It should also assume that user-facing package documentation belongs elsewhere:
- `README`
- roxygen/man
- vignettes/articles
- pkgdown site

---

# 4. Artifact roles

The module should define the purpose of each artifact.

## `dev/05_plan.md`
Canonical active work plan.

Use for:
- milestones
- ordered tasks
- definitions of done
- near-term progress

## `dev/10_design.md`
Stable current-state architecture and design document.

Use for:
- system/package structure
- invariants
- capability boundaries
- document map
- current design truths

## `dev/decisions/`
Architecture/design decision records.

Use for:
- major design choices
- rationale
- alternatives
- supersession history

## `dev/instructions/`
Chat/developer instruction modules and recipes.

Use for:
- reusable workflow instructions
- modular chat guidance
- development behavior conventions

## `dev/sessions/`
Archived chat/session transcripts.

Use for:
- transparency
- historical derivation
- audit trail

But not as the canonical source of final design state.

## Optional `dev/40_schemas.md`
Exact structural contracts.

Use for:
- file schemas
- structured object contracts
- required fields/types
- interface/data invariants

Only when warranted by the package.

---

# 5. Document precedence

This module should establish precedence rules.

When documents disagree, priority should be:

1. latest accepted decision record in `dev/decisions/` for the decision itself
2. `dev/10_design.md` for current architecture/state
3. `dev/40_schemas.md` for exact structural/schema contracts
4. `dev/05_plan.md` for intended next work
5. `dev/instructions/` for chat workflow behavior
6. `dev/sessions/` for historical context only

This gives chats a clear rule for resolving ambiguity.

---

# 6. Core behavior required from chat sessions

This is the heart of the module.

The module should instruct chat sessions to:

## A. Monitor for documentation triggers
Throughout the session, detect when new information should be promoted into durable docs.

## B. Identify the correct artifact(s)
When a trigger occurs, decide whether the update belongs in:
- plan
- design
- ADR
- schema doc
- instruction module

## C. Provide paste-ready updates
Do not merely recommend “document this later.”
Provide the user with concrete paste-ready content.

## D. Prefer concise final-state documentation
Summarize and distill decisions rather than telling the user to save the raw transcript.

## E. Treat documentation promotion as part of completion
If important decisions were made, the session should not act as though the work is fully wrapped up without surfacing the needed `dev/` updates.

---

# 7. Trigger conditions

This module should define specific triggers that require documentation updates.

## Update `dev/05_plan.md` when:
- new implementation work is identified
- milestones are refined
- task order changes materially
- definitions of done are clarified
- follow-up work is created from a design conversation

## Update `dev/10_design.md` when:
- package/repo architecture is defined or changed
- stable capability boundaries are clarified
- workflow responsibilities are clarified
- invariants or operating assumptions are established
- document structure/roles are redefined

## Create/update `dev/decisions/` when:
- a meaningful design choice is made
- alternatives were considered
- a convention is adopted intentionally
- an earlier decision is superseded or narrowed

## Update `dev/40_schemas.md` when:
- exact file/object/data contracts are defined
- required fields or structures become part of the design
- function outputs or scaffolded file structures need explicit schemas

## Update `dev/instructions/` when:
- reusable chat behavior is defined
- a new module/recipe is being designed
- workflow guidance should be preserved for future sessions

---

# 8. Completion criteria

This module should require that a session not silently leave important governance work undone.

Recommended completion rule:

A meaningful development task is not fully complete if the session has introduced or changed:
- architecture
- durable requirements
- design decisions
- structural contracts
- reusable workflow guidance

without also drafting the corresponding `dev/` updates for the user.

This does **not** mean every reply must update docs.
It means chats must recognize when promotion is warranted.

---

# 9. Anti-patterns to forbid

This module should explicitly discourage:

## 1. Treating session transcripts as the canonical source of truth
Transcripts are archival, not final-state docs.

## 2. Leaving decisions undocumented
If a design choice materially affects future work, capture it.

## 3. Mixing artifact roles
Do not use:
- plan files for architecture
- ADRs for task tracking
- session files as design docs
- vignettes as internal decision logs

## 4. Producing vague documentation advice
Do not just say “you should document this.”
Provide paste-ready content.

## 5. Over-documenting trivial changes
Do not create ADRs or design updates for every minor edit.

## 6. Duplicating the same material across all artifacts
Each document should have a distinct role.

---

# 10. Relationship to pkgdown/user-facing docs

This module should explicitly say:

For R packages, internal development-governance artifacts in `dev/` are distinct from user-facing package documentation.

Use pkgdown/vignettes/articles for:
- user guidance
- examples
- published workflows
- package functionality communication

Use `dev/` artifacts for:
- internal development state
- architecture
- design decisions
- session-governance continuity

This is important so chats do not try to put internal design decisions into public-facing docs by default.

---

# 11. Relationship to scaffolding functions

This module should assume that `reproducibleai` may provide functions to scaffold the governance framework, but the module itself governs **behavior during chat sessions**, not file creation mechanics.

That means:
- scaffolding functions create the structure
- this module governs how sessions maintain it

That boundary keeps the module focused.

---

# 12. Recommended output style from chats

The module should likely require chats to:

- clearly say which artifact(s) need updating
- explain briefly why
- provide paste-ready replacement/addition text
- separate recommended updates by artifact
- prefer concise, maintainable wording over transcript-like prose

That output style is essential for usability.

---

# 13. Suggested module structure

When written as an actual instruction module, I’d structure it like this:

1. Purpose
2. Scope and boundaries
3. Artifact roles
4. Document precedence
5. Trigger conditions
6. Required chat behavior
7. Completion rule
8. Anti-patterns
9. Output expectations

That should make it composable and readable.

---

This outline is now strong enough to turn into an actual `dev/instructions/` module.

I recommend we now draft the **actual module text** in the style you want `reproducibleai` to use, so it can later be added to:

- `dev/instructions/modules/development-governance.md`
or whatever naming/layout convention your package uses

## Instruction module draft

Use this as the proposed module text.

````markdown name=development-governance.md
# Development Governance

## Purpose
This module governs how AI-assisted development sessions maintain durable project documentation in repositories that use a `dev/` folder for development-state artifacts.

The goal is to ensure that important outcomes from a chat session are promoted into concise, structured repository documents rather than being left only in a session transcript.

This module is about:
- documentation governance
- artifact roles
- document precedence
- session-to-document promotion
- required chat behavior when durable project state changes

This module is not about:
- domain-specific implementation details
- general software engineering beyond documentation governance
- user-facing package documentation except where governance boundaries must be clarified

## When to use
Use this module when:
- the repository is developed through iterative AI-assisted chat sessions
- the repository uses a `dev/` directory to store development-state documentation
- the team wants durable records of plans, architecture, decisions, and reusable chat instructions
- the session may produce requirements, architecture, workflow, or schema decisions that should guide future work

This module is especially appropriate for:
- R packages
- `golem`-based Shiny apps developed as R packages
- other package-style repositories using `reproducibleai` conventions

## Assumed repository structure
This module assumes the repository may contain the following internal development-governance artifacts:

- `dev/05_plan.md`
- `dev/10_design.md`
- `dev/decisions/`
- `dev/instructions/`
- `dev/sessions/`
- optionally `dev/40_schemas.md`

It also assumes that user-facing package documentation belongs elsewhere, such as:
- `README`
- roxygen/man documentation
- vignettes/articles
- pkgdown site pages

## Artifact roles

### `dev/05_plan.md`
Canonical active work plan.

Use for:
- milestones
- ordered tasks
- definitions of done
- near-term progress and next steps

### `dev/10_design.md`
Stable current-state design and architecture document.

Use for:
- current package or system structure
- invariants
- capability boundaries
- document map
- stable operating assumptions
- current design truths that future work should inherit

### `dev/decisions/`
Decision records.

Use for:
- meaningful design choices
- rationale
- alternatives considered
- supersession history

### `dev/instructions/`
Reusable chat/developer instruction modules and recipes.

Use for:
- modular workflow instructions
- reusable chat behavior conventions
- recipe-oriented guidance for future sessions

### `dev/sessions/`
Archived session transcripts.

Use for:
- transparency
- historical derivation trail
- auditability

Do not treat session files as the canonical source of final design state.

### Optional `dev/40_schemas.md`
Exact structural contracts.

Use for:
- file schemas
- structured object contracts
- required fields and types
- interface/data invariants

Only use this document when the repository has enough structural complexity to justify a centralized schema reference.

## Document precedence
When development-governance artifacts disagree, apply this precedence:

1. the latest accepted decision record in `dev/decisions/` for the decision itself
2. `dev/10_design.md` for current architecture and stable design state
3. `dev/40_schemas.md` for exact structural contracts
4. `dev/05_plan.md` for intended next work
5. `dev/instructions/` for chat workflow behavior
6. `dev/sessions/` for historical context only

If ambiguity remains, the chat should call out the ambiguity explicitly and recommend which artifact should be updated to resolve it.

## Trigger conditions
During the session, monitor for changes that should be promoted into durable documentation.

### Update `dev/05_plan.md` when:
- new implementation work is identified
- milestones are refined
- task ordering changes materially
- definitions of done are clarified
- a design discussion creates follow-up work

### Update `dev/10_design.md` when:
- architecture is defined or changed
- stable capability boundaries are clarified
- package responsibilities are clarified
- invariants or operating assumptions are established
- document structure or governance roles are revised

### Create or update `dev/decisions/` when:
- a meaningful design choice is made
- alternatives were considered
- a convention is intentionally adopted
- an earlier design decision is superseded, narrowed, or replaced

### Update `dev/40_schemas.md` when:
- exact file, object, or data contracts are defined
- required fields or structures become part of the design
- outputs or scaffolded artifacts require a precise schema reference

### Update `dev/instructions/` when:
- reusable chat behavior is defined
- a new module or recipe is being designed
- guidance should be preserved for future sessions

## Required chat behavior
When a trigger condition is met, the chat must:

1. recognize that a documentation update is warranted
2. identify the correct artifact or artifacts to update
3. provide concise, paste-ready content for the user
4. prefer distilled final-state wording over transcript-like prose
5. remind the user when important governance updates remain pending

Do not merely say that something “should be documented later” when the session can already draft the correct update.

## Completion rule
A meaningful development task is not fully complete if the session has introduced or changed:
- architecture
- durable requirements
- design decisions
- structural contracts
- reusable workflow guidance

without also drafting the corresponding `dev/` updates for the user.

This does not require every reply to propose documentation edits. It requires the session to promote important durable changes before wrapping up.

## Anti-patterns
Avoid the following:

### 1. Treating session transcripts as the canonical source of truth
Session files are archival context, not final-state documentation.

### 2. Leaving meaningful decisions undocumented
If a design choice materially affects future work, capture it in the appropriate artifact.

### 3. Mixing artifact roles
Do not use:
- plan files for stable architecture
- ADRs for task tracking
- session files as design documents
- user-facing articles as internal decision logs

### 4. Giving vague documentation advice
Do not say only “you should document this.”
Provide paste-ready additions or replacements.

### 5. Over-documenting trivial changes
Do not create design updates or ADRs for every minor edit. Use judgment and focus on durable project state.

### 6. Duplicating the same material across all artifacts
Each document should have a distinct role. Summarize where appropriate and avoid copy-pasting large repeated sections across plan, design, and decision records.

## Relationship to user-facing package documentation
For R packages, internal development-governance artifacts in `dev/` are distinct from user-facing package documentation.

Use pkgdown/vignettes/articles for:
- user guidance
- package workflows
- examples
- published usage documentation

Use `dev/` artifacts for:
- internal development state
- architecture
- design decisions
- AI-assisted workflow continuity

Do not default to putting internal architecture decisions into public-facing docs unless those decisions are also useful to package users.

## Relationship to scaffolding functions
This module governs behavior during chat sessions. It does not require the chat to create files automatically.

`reproducibleai` may provide scaffolding functions to create the `dev/` governance structure, but this module is responsible for ensuring that sessions maintain and promote the contents of that structure correctly.

## Output expectations
When proposing governance updates, the chat should:
- clearly identify which artifact(s) need updating
- briefly explain why
- provide paste-ready content
- separate updates by artifact
- prefer concise, maintainable wording
- avoid leaving important decisions undocumented at the end of the session
````

## Recommended `dev/10_design.md` addition for `reproducibleai`

I recommend adding a section like this to `reproducibleai`’s design doc so the package itself records the governance capability.

````markdown name=dev-10-design-addition.md
## Development-governance capability

### Purpose
`reproducibleai` standardizes a development-governance framework for AI-assisted repositories that use iterative chat sessions as part of normal development work.

The goal is to make important outcomes from chat sessions durable, concise, and reviewable by promoting them into structured repository artifacts rather than leaving them only in archived transcripts.

### Governance model
The package assumes that internal development-state artifacts live in a repository `dev/` directory, while user-facing package documentation is communicated through standard package surfaces such as:
- `README`
- roxygen/man pages
- vignettes/articles
- pkgdown site pages

### Standard internal artifacts
The governance framework centers on these artifacts:

- `dev/05_plan.md` for active work planning
- `dev/10_design.md` for stable current-state architecture and design
- `dev/decisions/` for decision records
- `dev/instructions/` for modular chat/developer guidance
- `dev/sessions/` for archived transcripts

An optional `dev/40_schemas.md` may be used when exact structural contracts need centralized documentation.

### Behavioral enforcement
This capability is enforced primarily through instruction modules rather than through heavy automation.

The intended workflow is:
1. scaffold the governance structure in the repository
2. use instruction modules to condition chat sessions to monitor for governance-update triggers
3. require the chat session to provide paste-ready updates to the relevant `dev/` artifacts when durable project state changes

### Scope boundaries
This governance capability is distinct from domain-specific package functionality.

It governs:
- documentation roles
- document precedence
- session-to-document promotion
- durable design capture

It does not govern:
- package-specific implementation logic
- user-facing instructional content except where governance boundaries must be clarified

### Package responsibilities
`reproducibleai` should support this capability through:
- scaffolding functions that establish the `dev/` governance structure
- instruction modules that define chat behavior for maintaining it
- published package documentation that explains the governance framework to users
````

---

## Recommended ADR draft for `reproducibleai`

I recommend a concise ADR that records the decision to adopt development governance as a first-class package capability.

````markdown name=adr-development-governance.md
# ADR-XXXX: Adopt development-governance as a first-class `reproducibleai` capability

## Status
Accepted

## Context
`reproducibleai` is intended to support reproducible AI-assisted workflows across repositories and chat sessions. In practice, long-form AI-assisted development work produces important requirements, architectural decisions, workflow conventions, and implementation plans that should not remain only in chat transcripts.

The team has found that a repository-local `dev/` framework with separate documents for plan, design, decisions, instructions, and session archives provides strong continuity across sessions and helps maintain a concise record of project state.

## Decision
`reproducibleai` will adopt development governance as a first-class package capability.

This capability will standardize:
- the use of `dev/` artifacts for internal project governance
- the distinction between internal governance documents and user-facing package documentation
- instruction modules that require chat sessions to promote important durable outcomes into structured repository artifacts
- scaffolding support for establishing the governance structure in new repositories

For R packages, user-facing workflow and operational guidance should normally be published through vignettes/articles and pkgdown rather than through a separate internal runbook.

## Consequences
### Positive
- durable capture of architecture, decisions, and work plans
- improved continuity across chat sessions
- less reliance on raw transcripts as the source of truth
- reusable governance conventions across team repositories

### Tradeoffs
- requires deliberate maintenance of `dev/` artifacts
- introduces more structured documentation conventions than a minimal package workflow
- relies on instruction-module compliance by chat sessions and developers

## Notes
This decision establishes the governance framework as separate from package-specific functional modules such as parameterized help for `golem`-based Shiny apps.
````

I recommend you adopt this in `reproducibleai` in this order:

1. add the **development-governance instruction module**
2. update `dev/10_design.md` with the governance capability section
3. add the ADR
4. then design the scaffolding function for the governance framework
5. then return to the parameterized-help module within that broader governance system

If you want, next I can draft the **scaffolding function specification** for this governance capability, or I can draft the **parameterized-help instruction module** in the same style now that the governance foundation is defined.

## Copilot

Yes — now that development-governance is defined, the next logical step is the **parameterized-help instruction module**.

## Direct recommendation

This module should be scoped narrowly as:

> **Parameterized contextual help for `golem`-based Shiny apps developed as R packages**

That keeps it:
- compatible with your modular recipe approach
- distinct from the governance module
- implementation-oriented enough to be reproducible
- aligned with how you actually build Shiny apps

---

# Proposed module purpose

This module should govern how a Shiny app implements a reusable, maintainable, package-based contextual help system using structured help data, stable IDs, composable records, helper functions, and CSS support for rich popovers.

It should tell future chat sessions:
- how to structure help content
- where it lives
- how it is rendered
- how to compose it
- how to validate and maintain it
- how to keep it aligned with actual app behavior

---

# Proposed scope boundary

## This module should govern
- structured help-data architecture
- required help-data fields and storage conventions
- stable help IDs
- composition of help records into UI surfaces
- distinction between overview vs granular help
- package-data workflow for help content
- CSS expectations for long/complex popovers
- validation and drift review expectations
- use of `reproducibleai` helpers/scaffolding where applicable

## This module should not govern
- general Shiny UI architecture
- domain content for the app
- overall app navigation
- generic CSS beyond help-specific needs
- business logic
- general package governance

That keeps it composable alongside:
- `development-governance`
- `r-package`
- `shiny-golem`

---

# Recommended assumptions

This module should explicitly assume:

- the app is built with `golem`
- the app is an R package
- help content is package data
- user-facing help content is part of app functionality, not ad hoc prose
- runtime helper functions come from `reproducibleai`, rather than being copied into each app repo by default

That is important because your clarified architecture relies on imported helpers rather than local helper clones.

---

# Proposed architecture to encode in the module

## 1. Help content as structured package data
Use a dataset with required columns:
- `id`
- `title`
- `summary`
- `detail`

## 2. Help data creation convention
Create/edit help content in:
- `data-raw/create_help_data.R`

Persist as package data:
- `data/help_data.rda`

Document dataset in:
- `R/help_data.R`

## 3. Stable ID contract
UI code must reference help by stable IDs, not inline prose.

## 4. Overview + granular records
Support both:
- overview records for surfaces like tabs/plots/tables
- granular records for variables, metrics, and concepts

## 5. Composable rendering
Use:
- `reproducibleai::compose_help_items()`
- `reproducibleai::render_help_items()`

or imported equivalents

## 6. Context-specific help surfaces
Distinguish roles for:
- intro/getting-started help
- tab/sidebar help
- plot help
- table help
- variable-level help

## 7. CSS support
Help popovers that contain multiple records must support:
- wider max width
- scrollable body
- readable long-form content

## 8. Formula policy
Use block MathJax for formulas.
Avoid inline math where it makes rendering brittle.

## 9. Maintenance expectations
Require:
- help-ID integrity review
- drift review after functionality changes
- alignment with implemented behavior, not aspirational behavior

---

# Key behavioral rules for the module

This module should teach future chats to:

## During implementation
- scaffold or verify the package-data help framework
- propose help architecture before writing lots of prose
- create granular records for reusable concepts
- compose records into UI surfaces instead of duplicating prose
- prefer reusable help records over one-off UI text

## During refinement
- check for missing referenced IDs
- identify orphaned but useful records
- tighten overlap between help surfaces
- distinguish overview/help-surface text from detailed technical definitions

## During maintenance
- update help when app behavior changes
- revise formulas and units to match code
- flag stale help records after renames/refactors
- provide paste-ready updates where documentation drift is found

---

# Anti-patterns to forbid

This module should explicitly discourage:

## 1. Inline hard-coded help prose scattered across UI/server code
Help should be data-driven.

## 2. Duplicating the same explanation in many places
Use composable records.

## 3. Letting help describe intended behavior instead of implemented behavior
Help must match code.

## 4. Using unstable or ad hoc IDs
IDs are the contract.

## 5. Treating all help surfaces the same
Each surface should have a distinct role.

## 6. Using long popovers without CSS support
This harms usability.

## 7. Mixing package scaffolding with runtime helper duplication
Apps should import helper functions from `reproducibleai` by default rather than copying them locally.

---

# Recommended completion rule

A parameterized-help task should not be considered complete if the session has:
- introduced or changed help architecture
- added or renamed help IDs
- changed help-related outputs/variables
- changed plotted or displayed metrics
- altered contextual-help composition

without also:
- updating the relevant help data
- checking integrity of referenced IDs
- surfacing paste-ready updates for the user

---

# Suggested actual module structure

I recommend the module itself be organized as:

1. Purpose
2. When to use
3. Scope and boundaries
4. Assumptions
5. Required architecture
6. Required conventions
7. Required chat behavior
8. Maintenance triggers
9. Anti-patterns
10. Completion rule

That mirrors the governance module and should fit your recipe system well.
